        Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

User notes and answers:
- Resolved plan questions: (1) do not hard-code eu-no-1/eur-no-1; derive canonical prebuilt volume names from the actual RunPod data_center_id normalization and have docs mention the observed reference volume only as an example. (2) Required first remote smoke gate should be cheapest z_image_turbo, then broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix runs after base health is proven. (3) SageAttention should be an optional validated profile: implement/install/check support when requested, but portable 4090 validation must remain the default and must not be blocked by Sage installation failure unless the user explicitly requests the sage profile.
- T13 manually validated after fixing prebuilt check probe extraction/system-tools/min-memory and reconciling selected Z-Image assets. Evidence: runpod-lifecycle prebuilt check --data-center EUR-NO-1 --attention-profile portable --gpu-type 'NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090' --targets-json /tmp/reigh-z-image-turbo-targets-main.json --enriched-targets-json /tmp/reigh-z-image-turbo-enriched-prebuilt.json exited 0. It launched pod 7elgasx2c0168d, extracted runtime, ran health probes, wrote /workspace/reigh-livetest-prebuilt/env.health.json with ok=true and only runtime_deferred informational issue, then terminated the pod.
- T14 manual evidence: prebuilt VibeComfy smoke ran on RunPod pod 4szlq6112u86wn using volume reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1. Worker became ready, task 63e047e8-acb1-4538-994b-87acec85f05a completed for z_image_turbo, and output artifact was written to Supabase storage. Reconstructed report after live-test reporting fix is scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T062551Z/report.json with passed=1 total=1, final_status=Complete, generation_id=23bfe12f-c53e-4956-a039-bb5cfa810084, and no error_summary. Validation pod was cleaned up; subsequent runpod-lifecycle list showed only persistent user pod cr9qblqcq1loag.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

## Overview
The latest critique does not change the root approach or target code. It identifies a caller/artifact gap in the JSON handoff: standalone `rl prebuilt check --targets-json ...` needs a target JSON producer before any RunPod pod is launched, and no-credential `reconcile --dry-run` cannot promise an asset fetch plan from a plain target manifest unless enrichment has already happened or a local VibeComfy enrichment command is explicitly available.

The plan stays aligned with the existing architecture. `reigh-worker` owns live-test case/route selection and adds a target-manifest-only mode. `vibecomfy` owns source/schema/asset enrichment through its own CLI. `runpod-lifecycle` owns RunPod pod/volume lifecycle and consumes JSON artifacts. The dry-run contract becomes explicit: with enriched metadata it can print asset fetches; with plain targets and no local enrichment path, it exits early with the exact command needed to produce enriched metadata.

## Phase 1: Contracts, Builder Enforcement, And Selection

### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, observed Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and profile-specific checks.
2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field, but require builder verification before writing it.
3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.
4. Keep manifest compatibility evidence separate from generated health evidence.

### Step 2: Enforce worker Python in the builder (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `_uv_sync_builder_shell()` to accept the desired worker Python version and pass it to `uv sync` through an explicit `--python <version>` or equivalent `uv venv --python <version>` bootstrap before sync.
2. After worker install, run the built worker venv Python and compare observed `major.minor` to `contract.python_version` before `_cmd_prebuilt_build` constructs `PrebuiltManifest`.
3. Fail the build before bundle/archive/manifest write if the observed worker interpreter does not match the requested manifest Python version.
4. Record the observed worker interpreter in `PrebuiltHealthReport.worker_env` and keep the manifest field trustworthy.

### Step 3: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.
2. Add `--prebuilt-data-center` for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.
3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.
4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example.

## Phase 2: JSON Handoff And VibeComfy Enrichment

### Step 4: Add a standalone Reigh target manifest producer (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a no-pod target emission mode, for example `python -m scripts.live_test.main --emit-targets-json <path> --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo ...`, that builds the selected live-test cases and writes target JSON without touching DB, RunPod, or worker launch paths.
2. Reuse the same resolver in normal `--variant prebuilt` execution so standalone checks and live consumer runs validate the same selected targets.
3. The target JSON contains selected case names, task types, route keys, selector/profile context, and resolved VibeComfy template ids only.
4. Do not include VibeComfy-derived asset metadata in this Reigh step, and do not import `vibecomfy` from `reigh-worker`.
5. Ensure the target set is derived from selected live-test cases/routes, not all VibeComfy ready templates.

### Step 5: Expose workflow source mode in named VibeComfy APIs (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py`, VibeComfy tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a named source-mode enum or string field such as `python_composition`, `api_dict_wrapper`, or `json_reference` to the ready-template registry/loader surface that currently backs `ready_template_ids()` and `workflow_from_ready()`.
2. Implement source classification in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py` or an adjacent helper used by that registry, and expose it through a function such as `ready_template_source_info(template_id)`.
3. Use `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py` only as a loader integration point; do not make callers infer source mode from compiled workflow dictionaries.
4. Add diagnostics that name the template id and source file when a selected app-active template is still an API-dict/JSON runtime wrapper.

### Step 6: Add VibeComfy-owned enrichment CLI (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/...`, VibeComfy asset helpers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a VibeComfy CLI command or subcommand that accepts Reigh’s target manifest JSON and emits an enriched validation manifest containing template source mode, schema-check inputs, expected model assets, asset categories/subdirectories, known URLs, and expected relative locations.
2. Support explicit output, for example `python -m vibecomfy.cli targets enrich --targets-json reigh-targets.json --out enriched-targets.json`.
3. Run this command with VibeComfy’s own Python environment: locally only when a VibeComfy checkout/python is explicitly supplied, or remotely inside `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv/bin/python` after the prebuilt VibeComfy checkout has been extracted/synced.
4. Keep `reigh-worker` as a JSON producer/consumer only. It may invoke the VibeComfy CLI as a subprocess when an explicit VibeComfy Python/check-out path is available, but it must not import VibeComfy modules.
5. Keep `runpod-lifecycle` as a JSON consumer/remote-command runner only. It must not import Reigh or VibeComfy package APIs locally.

### Step 7: Define enriched asset diagnostics and dry-run contracts (`vibecomfy` enrichment output, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use the VibeComfy-enriched manifest as the source of expected model assets for selected templates.
2. Report missing assets with asset name, category/path, local paths checked through `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and known remediation when available.
3. Keep `check` non-mutating by default; it can print the exact reconcile command using the enriched manifest.
4. Define `rl prebuilt reconcile --dry-run` precisely: with `--asset-manifest` or an enriched `--targets-json`, print planned fetches; with a plain `--targets-json`, require explicit local enrichment options such as `--vibecomfy-python` and `--vibecomfy-root`, otherwise exit with a precise remediation command.
5. Non-dry-run reconcile may enrich remotely on a probe pod because credentials/network are allowed in that mode.

## Phase 3: Shared Probes And CLI Surface

### Step 8: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Refactor `verify_extracted_env()` into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, and optional enriched target/asset manifest data.
2. Probe the actual remote paths separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` for worker and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv` for VibeComfy.
3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only for `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, selected source mode, selected node schemas, and selected model assets.
4. Return structured issue objects grouped by `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.

### Step 9: Add prebuilt check/status/cleanup/reconcile parser and dispatch (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update the prebuilt dispatch map to include `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile` in addition to `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, and `list`.
2. Add parser entries and help text for all new subcommands.
3. Require `--dry-run` support for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`; dry-run must not require `RUNPOD_API_KEY` or provision a pod.
4. Add `--targets-json`, `--asset-manifest`, and explicit local enrichment options where needed. Do not add Reigh-owned `--case` or `--route-key` flags to `runpod-lifecycle`.
5. Add `--json`, `--attention-profile portable|sage`, `--gpu-type`, `--container-disk-gb`, and `--keep-pod` where applicable.

### Step 10: Implement standalone check and reconcile with clean package boundaries (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. `rl prebuilt check` provisions a clearly named short-lived probe pod, attaches the selected volume, reads the manifest, extracts or inspects bundles as needed, runs the VibeComfy enrichment command remotely when a plain target manifest is supplied, runs shared in-pod probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates by default.
2. `rl prebuilt check --dry-run` with a plain target manifest prints planned pod/volume/probe steps and says enrichment will occur remotely during non-dry-run; it does not need to print asset fetches.
3. `rl prebuilt reconcile` accepts an enriched asset manifest or runs remote VibeComfy enrichment first, then runs remote VibeComfy fetch/stage commands inside the pod checkout or stages known URLs directly into the model cache.
4. `rl prebuilt reconcile --dry-run` only prints asset fetch plans when given enriched metadata or explicit local enrichment options; otherwise it exits successfully or with code 2, as chosen by local CLI convention, with the exact enrichment command needed before retrying dry-run.
5. Neither command imports Reigh routing code or VibeComfy Python modules locally.
6. `--keep-pod` is explicit and includes pod id/reason in output when used.

### Step 11: Add prebuilt status and cleanup safety (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `rl prebuilt status --dry-run --json` that only lists pods matching explicit allowlisted prefixes: `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`.
2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only terminates allowlisted prebuilt validation pods and never generic user pods.
3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning logic where practical, but keep the prebuilt allowlist visible and unit-tested.

## Phase 4: Live-Test Integration And Reports

### Step 12: Validate the actual consumer pod before queueing (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After manifest read, hard-fail drift checks, extraction, worker git sync, VibeComfy git sync/delta install, and `_write_extra_model_paths_yaml()`, call the shared in-pod health probe directly on that same SSH session.
2. Generate the same Reigh target manifest used by `--emit-targets-json`, then run VibeComfy enrichment via the remote VibeComfy Python/checkout on the consumer pod before asset/schema checks.
3. Do not shell out to `rl prebuilt check` from inside `variant_prebuilt`; the CLI validates a separate probe pod and is only for standalone volume checks.
4. Abort before `register_worker_record()`, `launch_worker`, or `queue_matrix()` when health checks find environment, pure-Python source, schema, required input/connection, or asset failures.
5. Write the health summary, Reigh target manifest, and enriched VibeComfy manifest to the live-test run directory even on early failure when possible.

### Step 13: Add optional report metadata without breaking other variants (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, fresh/update call sites)
**Scope:** Small
1. Change `write_report(results, variant, pod_id, out_dir)` to accept an optional keyword-only metadata parameter, for example `write_report(..., metadata=None)`.
2. Have prebuilt pass volume id/name, data center, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, target manifest path/hash, enriched manifest path/hash, selected profile, selected templates, GPU type, and check summary.
3. Leave fresh/update callers valid with no changes beyond the compatible function signature.

## Phase 5: Tests And Documentation

### Step 14: Add focused tests for builder, contracts, and CLI (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test that the builder sync command pins the requested worker Python version and fails before manifest write on observed mismatch.
2. Test health report JSON round trip, separate worker/VibeComfy interpreter records, missing asset diagnostics, and schema-check issue grouping.
3. Update existing prebuilt help tests so they expect all subcommands: `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, `list`, `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
4. Add no-credential dry-run tests for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`, including reconcile behavior with plain targets, enriched metadata, and explicit local enrichment options.
5. Add deterministic volume-selection tests for explicit volume name, explicit data center, multiple matching candidates, and canonical normalized names.

### Step 15: Add VibeComfy and live-test boundary tests (`vibecomfy/tests/...`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test `ready_template_source_info()` or equivalent on pure Python templates, API-dict wrapper templates, and JSON reference/corpus paths.
2. Test the VibeComfy enrichment CLI: target manifest in, enriched source/schema/asset manifest out.
3. Test `--emit-targets-json` produces a standalone target file for `z_image_turbo` and other selected cases without DB, RunPod, pod launch, or VibeComfy imports.
4. Test that Reigh target manifest generation resolves selected live-test cases/routes and excludes unrelated ready templates without importing `vibecomfy`.
5. Add a static/import-boundary test that fails if `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` directly imports `vibecomfy` for target or asset manifest generation.
6. Test that prebuilt health failure occurs before worker registration, launch, and matrix queueing.
7. Test that optional report metadata preserves existing fresh/update report behavior.
8. Test that `runpod-lifecycle reconcile` consumes resolved/enriched metadata and does not parse Reigh `--case` or `--route-key` flags.

### Step 16: Document the operational workflow (`runpod-lifecycle/README.md`, nearest `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` docs, relevant VibeComfy docs)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document prebuilt vs fresh, default portable RTX 4090 behavior, optional larger GPU and SageAttention profiles, and canonical volume naming from RunPod `data_center_id` normalization.
2. Document `build`, `check`, `reconcile`, `status`, `cleanup`, `invalidate`, and `list`, including dry-run/no-credential behavior and the `--targets-json`/`--asset-manifest` handoff.
3. Document the explicit producer sequence: Reigh `--emit-targets-json`, optional VibeComfy local enrichment, then `rl prebuilt check` or `rl prebuilt reconcile`.
4. Document reconcile dry-run behavior for plain targets versus enriched manifests.
5. Document workflow onboarding: derive/convert/fork pure Python workflow, validate selected route/template schemas, verify assets, run cheap prebuilt check, run `z_image_turbo` smoke, then broaden to image/edit/upscale or video matrices.
6. State that app-active runtime workflow source must be pure Python; JSON/API-dict wrappers are allowed only as reference/import/corpus material.

## Phase 6: Validation

### Step 17: Run local verification
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted tests first: `pytest runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py` plus the VibeComfy source/enrichment tests.
2. Run broader affected suites after targeted tests pass.

### Step 18: Battle-test on RunPod
**Scope:** Large
1. Generate the target manifest first: `python -m scripts.live_test.main --emit-targets-json <reigh-targets.json> --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo`.
2. Discover the actual current portable prebuilt volume and data center from RunPod instead of hard-coding the historical example.
3. Run `rl prebuilt check --targets-json <reigh-targets.json>` against the selected portable 4090 profile and confirm remote VibeComfy enrichment plus `env.health.json` evidence are written.
4. Run the first remote smoke gate with `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py --variant prebuilt --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo` using the selected volume/profile.
5. Confirm pods launched by check/smoke are terminated unless intentionally preserved with `--keep-pod`, and record any preserved pod id and reason.
6. After base health and smoke pass, run broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix validation as a follow-up gate, not as the first blocker.

## Execution Order
1. Enforce builder worker Python version before trusting or expanding manifest/health evidence.
2. Add deterministic volume selection before wiring new commands that depend on volume/profile names.
3. Add Reigh `--emit-targets-json` so standalone validation has an explicit artifact producer.
4. Add VibeComfy source-mode API and VibeComfy-owned enrichment CLI before asset reconciliation.
5. Add parser/dispatch/dry-run behavior for new CLI verbs with precise plain-target versus enriched-manifest contracts.
6. Wire remote VibeComfy enrichment and shared in-pod probes into `variant_prebuilt` after target metadata and probes are stable.
7. Add report metadata compatibility and docs after command/output shape is settled.

## Validation Order
1. Start with builder Python enforcement and dry-run/no-credential CLI tests.
2. Run unit tests for target JSON emission, health report serialization, volume selection, source classification, VibeComfy enrichment, target-manifest handoff, asset diagnostics, import-boundary checks, and cleanup allowlists.
3. Run live-test integration tests proving early failure before queueing.
4. Run broader affected local tests.
5. Generate `reigh-targets.json`, then run real `rl prebuilt check --targets-json ...` on RunPod.
6. Run real `z_image_turbo` prebuilt smoke and verify report evidence plus pod cleanup.

    "The existing `rl prebuilt build/inspect/invalidate/list` and `reigh-worker --variant prebuilt|auto` flow should be extended, not replaced.",

    "Dry-run paths for new commands must not require RunPod credentials or make network calls."

  "orchestrator_guidance": "Force-proceed override applied. Proceed to finalize.",
  "robustness": "standard",
  "signals": {
    "iteration": 5,
    "idea": "# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\nWe are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.\n\nCurrent reality:\n- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`\n- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`\n- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`\n- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.\n- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.\n- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.\n\nGoal:\nBuild and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.\n\nThe target design should:\n1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.\n   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.\n   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.\n   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.\n2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.\n   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.\n   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.\n3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.\n   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.\n   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.\n4. Make validation fail early.\n   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.\n   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.\n   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.\n5. Make pod lifecycle safe.\n   - Never kill unrelated user pods.\n   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.\n   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.\n6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.\n   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.\n   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.\n   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.\n7. Be documented for agents and developers.\n   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.\n   - Document the \"workflow onboarding\" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.\n\nExpected implementation shape:\n- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.\n- Add tests for the new behavior.\n- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.\n- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.\n- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.\n\nValidation requirements:\n- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.\n- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.\n- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.\n- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.\n- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.\n\nKnown useful references already produced:\n- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.\n- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.\n\nImportant safety:\n- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.\n- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.\n- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.\n\nSuccess criteria:\n- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.\n- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.\n- Documentation explains both the \"why\" and the commands succinctly.\n- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.",
    "significant_flags": 0,
    "unresolved_flags": [],
    "resolved_flags": [
      {
        "id": "FLAG-001",
        "concern": "CLI contract: `rl prebuilt check` omits the required dry-run behavior from the implementation steps even though it is a must-level success criterion.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to address the gate feedback directly: added explicit dry-run/no-credential behavior for new CLI verbs, modeled worker and VibeComfy virtualenvs separately, required a shared in-pod probe for the actual consumer pod, derived app-active templates from selected Reigh live-test cases, added pure-Python source enforcement, added asset reconcile/fetch tooling, specified parser/dispatch/test updates, made report metadata backward-compatible, and added deterministic data-center-aware volume selection."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-002",
        "concern": "Environment contract: the health/manifest plan treats Python profile as a single value despite separate worker and VibeComfy virtualenvs that can use different interpreter versions.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to address the gate feedback directly: added explicit dry-run/no-credential behavior for new CLI verbs, modeled worker and VibeComfy virtualenvs separately, required a shared in-pod probe for the actual consumer pod, derived app-active templates from selected Reigh live-test cases, added pure-Python source enforcement, added asset reconcile/fetch tooling, specified parser/dispatch/test updates, made report metadata backward-compatible, and added deterministic data-center-aware volume selection."
      },
      {
        "id": "FLAG-003",
        "concern": "Workflow source-of-truth: the plan documents pure-Python app-active workflows but does not add an enforcement/classification gate for app-active routes that still resolve to API-dict wrapper templates.",
        "resolution": "Revised the plan to address the gate feedback directly: added explicit dry-run/no-credential behavior for new CLI verbs, modeled worker and VibeComfy virtualenvs separately, required a shared in-pod probe for the actual consumer pod, derived app-active templates from selected Reigh live-test cases, added pure-Python source enforcement, added asset reconcile/fetch tooling, specified parser/dispatch/test updates, made report metadata backward-compatible, and added deterministic data-center-aware volume selection."
      },
      {

      "commands_run": [
        "git status --short",
        "sed -n '1,220p' .megaplan/plans/prebuilt-vibecomfy-env-all-codex/user_actions.md",
        "for f in .env .env.local reigh-worker/.env reigh-worker/.env.local runpod-lifecycle/.env runpod-lifecycle/.env.local; do if [ -f \"$f\" ]; then if grep -q '^RUNPOD_API_KEY=' \"$f\"; then printf '%s RUNPOD_API_KEY_PRESENT\\n' \"$f\"; else printf '%s RUNPOD_API_KEY_ABSENT\\n' \"$f\"; fi; fi; done",
        "RUNPOD_API_KEY=\"$(awk -F= '/^RUNPOD_API_KEY=/{print substr($0,index($0,\"=\")+1); exit}' runpod-lifecycle/.env)\"; export RUNPOD_API_KEY; cd runpod-lifecycle && python -m runpod_lifecycle.cli volumes ls >/tmp/runpod_volumes_check.out 2>/tmp/runpod_volumes_check.err; rc=$?; if [ \"$rc\" -eq 0 ]; then printf 'RUNPOD_ACCESS_VERIFIED volumes_ls_ok lines=%s\\n' \"$(wc -l < /tmp/runpod_volumes_check.out | tr -d ' ')\"; else printf 'RUNPOD_ACCESS_VERIFY_FAILED exit=%s stderr_redacted=%s\\n' \"$rc\" \"$(sed -E 's/[A-Za-z0-9_=-]{20,}/[REDACTED]/g' /tmp/runpod_volumes_check.err | tr '\\n' ' ' | cut -c1-240)\"; fi; rm -f /tmp/runpod_volumes_check.out /tmp/runpod_volumes_check.err",
        "grep -Eq 'otherwise .*terminate|terminate.*default|terminated by default|cleanup' .megaplan/plans/prebuilt-vibecomfy-env-all-codex/user_actions.md && printf 'U2_DEFAULT_CLEANUP_DIRECTIVE_PRESENT\\n' || printf 'U2_DEFAULT_CLEANUP_DIRECTIVE_MISSING\\n'",

        Requirements:
        - Verify each success criterion explicitly.
        - Trust executor evidence by default. Dig deeper only where the git diff, `execution_audit.json`, or vague notes make the claim ambiguous.
        - Each criterion has a `priority` (`must`, `should`, or `info`). Apply these rules:
          - `must` criteria are hard gates. A `must` criterion that fails means `needs_rework`.
          - `should` criteria are quality targets. If the spirit is met but the letter is not, mark `pass` with evidence explaining the gap. Only mark `fail` if the intent was clearly missed. A `should` failure alone does NOT require `needs_rework`.
          - `info` criteria are for human reference. Mark them `waived` with a note — do not evaluate them.
          - If a criterion has `requires` capabilities that are not satisfiable by container workers (e.g., `drive_browser`, `subjective_judgment`), mark it `deferred_human` — NOT `fail` or `waived`. Deferred-human criteria do NOT count toward `needs_rework`.
          - If a criterion (any priority) cannot be verified in this context (e.g., requires manual testing or runtime observation), mark it `waived` with an explanation.
        - Set `review_verdict` to `needs_rework` only when at least one `must` criterion fails or actual implementation work is incomplete. Use `approved` when all `must` criteria pass, even if some `should` criteria are flagged.

        - baseline_test_failures in finalize.json lists tests that were already failing before execution. Do not flag these as rework items unless the executor introduced new failures in those same tests.
        - Cross-reference each task's `files_changed` and `commands_run` against the git diff and any audit findings.
        - Review every `sense_check` explicitly and treat perfunctory acknowledgments as a reason to dig deeper.
        - Follow this JSON shape exactly:
        ```json
        {
          "review_verdict": "approved",
          "criteria": [
            {
              "name": "All existing tests pass",
              "priority": "must",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "Test suite ran green — 42 passed, 0 failed."
            },
            {
              "name": "File under ~300 lines",
              "priority": "should",
              "pass": "pass",
              "evidence": "File is 375 lines — above the target but reasonable given the component's responsibilities. Spirit met."
            },
            {
              "name": "Manual smoke tests pass",
              "priority": "info",
              "pass": "waived",
              "evidence": "Cannot be verified in automated review. Noted for manual QA."
            }
          ],
          "issues": [],
          "rework_items": [],
          "summary": "Approved. All must criteria pass. The should criterion on line count is close enough given the component scope.",
          "task_verdicts": [
            {
              "task_id": "T6",
              "reviewer_verdict": "Pass. Claimed handler changes and command evidence match the repo state.",
              "evidence_files": ["megaplan/handlers.py", "megaplan/evaluation.py"]
            }
          ],
          "sense_check_verdicts": [
            {
              "sense_check_id": "SC6",
              "verdict": "Confirmed. The execute blocker only fires when both evidence arrays are empty."
            }
          ]
        }
        ```
        - `rework_items` must be an array of structured rework directives. When `review_verdict` is `needs_rework`, populate one entry per issue with:
          - `task_id`: which finalize task this issue relates to
          - `issue`: what is wrong
          - `expected`: what correct behavior looks like
          - `actual`: what was observed
          - `evidence_file` (optional): file path supporting the finding
          - `flag_id`: critique/review flag ID when applicable, otherwise `null`
          - `source`: short machine-readable source tag when applicable, otherwise `null`
        - `issues` must still be populated as a flat one-line-per-item summary derived from `rework_items` (for backward compatibility). When approved, both `issues` and `rework_items` should be empty arrays.
        - When the work needs another execute pass, keep the same shape and change only `review_verdict` to `needs_rework`; make `issues`, `rework_items`, `summary`, and task verdicts specific enough for the executor to act on directly.

        Idea:
# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

We are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.

Current reality:
- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`
- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`
- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`
- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.
- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.
- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.

Goal:
Build and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.

The target design should:
1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.
   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.
   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.
   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.
2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.
   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.
   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.
3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.
   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.
   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.
4. Make validation fail early.
   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.
   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.
   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.
5. Make pod lifecycle safe.
   - Never kill unrelated user pods.
   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.
   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.
6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.
   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.
   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.
   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.
7. Be documented for agents and developers.
   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.
   - Document the "workflow onboarding" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.

Expected implementation shape:
- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.
- Add tests for the new behavior.
- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.
- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.
- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.

Validation requirements:
- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.
- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.
- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.
- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.
- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.

Known useful references already produced:
- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.
- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.
- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.

Important safety:
- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.
- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.
- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.

Success criteria:
- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.
- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.
- Documentation explains both the "why" and the commands succinctly.
- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.

User notes and answers:
- Resolved plan questions: (1) do not hard-code eu-no-1/eur-no-1; derive canonical prebuilt volume names from the actual RunPod data_center_id normalization and have docs mention the observed reference volume only as an example. (2) Required first remote smoke gate should be cheapest z_image_turbo, then broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix runs after base health is proven. (3) SageAttention should be an optional validated profile: implement/install/check support when requested, but portable 4090 validation must remain the default and must not be blocked by Sage installation failure unless the user explicitly requests the sage profile.
- T13 manually validated after fixing prebuilt check probe extraction/system-tools/min-memory and reconciling selected Z-Image assets. Evidence: runpod-lifecycle prebuilt check --data-center EUR-NO-1 --attention-profile portable --gpu-type 'NVIDIA GeForce RTX 4090' --targets-json /tmp/reigh-z-image-turbo-targets-main.json --enriched-targets-json /tmp/reigh-z-image-turbo-enriched-prebuilt.json exited 0. It launched pod 7elgasx2c0168d, extracted runtime, ran health probes, wrote /workspace/reigh-livetest-prebuilt/env.health.json with ok=true and only runtime_deferred informational issue, then terminated the pod.
- T14 manual evidence: prebuilt VibeComfy smoke ran on RunPod pod 4szlq6112u86wn using volume reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1. Worker became ready, task 63e047e8-acb1-4538-994b-87acec85f05a completed for z_image_turbo, and output artifact was written to Supabase storage. Reconstructed report after live-test reporting fix is scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T062551Z/report.json with passed=1 total=1, final_status=Complete, generation_id=23bfe12f-c53e-4956-a039-bb5cfa810084, and no error_summary. Validation pod was cleaned up; subsequent runpod-lifecycle list showed only persistent user pod cr9qblqcq1loag.
- Review bookkeeping evidence: implementation changes are committed and pushed, so they are not visible in dirty git diff. runpod-lifecycle branch megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env at 3e99a54 contains README.md, src/runpod_lifecycle/__init__.py, src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py, src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py, tests/test_cli.py, tests/test_prebuilt.py. vibecomfy branch megaplan/production-parity-templates at de067c2 contains docs/adding_templates_models.md, tests/test_cli.py, tests/test_ready_templates.py, vibecomfy/commands/workflows.py, vibecomfy/registry/ready.py. reigh-worker branch megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix includes cde8fd81/6b82d712/6b82d712/64a63146/13b065fb/25d0c342 plus 7adaff35; 7adaff35 contains scripts/live_test/completion_poller.py, scripts/live_test/report.py, scripts/live_test/tests/test_primitives.py. Focused tests passed after 7adaff35: PYENV_VERSION=3.11.11 python -m pytest scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py scripts/live_test/tests/test_primitives.py -q => 156 passed. The final-diff review should verify commit ranges/branch heads, not only dirty worktree state.

        Approved plan:
        # Implementation Plan: Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment

## Overview
The latest critique does not change the root approach or target code. It identifies a caller/artifact gap in the JSON handoff: standalone `rl prebuilt check --targets-json ...` needs a target JSON producer before any RunPod pod is launched, and no-credential `reconcile --dry-run` cannot promise an asset fetch plan from a plain target manifest unless enrichment has already happened or a local VibeComfy enrichment command is explicitly available.

The plan stays aligned with the existing architecture. `reigh-worker` owns live-test case/route selection and adds a target-manifest-only mode. `vibecomfy` owns source/schema/asset enrichment through its own CLI. `runpod-lifecycle` owns RunPod pod/volume lifecycle and consumes JSON artifacts. The dry-run contract becomes explicit: with enriched metadata it can print asset fetches; with plain targets and no local enrichment path, it exits early with the exact command needed to produce enriched metadata.

## Phase 1: Contracts, Builder Enforcement, And Selection

### Step 1: Model the real prebuilt environment (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a versioned `PrebuiltHealthReport` and nested records for `worker_env` and `vibecomfy_env`, each recording executable path, observed Python version, Torch version, CUDA version, import status, and profile-specific checks.
2. Keep `PrebuiltManifest.python_version` as the worker bundle compatibility field, but require builder verification before writing it.
3. Add atomic JSON helpers for `env.health.json` next to `env.manifest.json`, preserving current SSH heredoc/write patterns.
4. Keep manifest compatibility evidence separate from generated health evidence.

### Step 2: Enforce worker Python in the builder (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update `_uv_sync_builder_shell()` to accept the desired worker Python version and pass it to `uv sync` through an explicit `--python <version>` or equivalent `uv venv --python <version>` bootstrap before sync.
2. After worker install, run the built worker venv Python and compare observed `major.minor` to `contract.python_version` before `_cmd_prebuilt_build` constructs `PrebuiltManifest`.
3. Fail the build before bundle/archive/manifest write if the observed worker interpreter does not match the requested manifest Python version.
4. Record the observed worker interpreter in `PrebuiltHealthReport.worker_env` and keep the manifest field trustworthy.

### Step 3: Normalize volume names and deterministic selection (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/config.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Centralize data-center normalization for canonical names such as `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-<normalized-data-center>` without hard-coding `eu-no-1` or `eur-no-1`.
2. Add `--prebuilt-data-center` for `--variant prebuilt|auto` and pass it into `_resolve_volume()` as a filter when supplied.
3. When multiple matching profile volumes exist and no data center/name is supplied, fail with a clear list of candidates instead of selecting the first arbitrary match.
4. In docs, mention `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` only as an observed historical example.

## Phase 2: JSON Handoff And VibeComfy Enrichment

### Step 4: Add a standalone Reigh target manifest producer (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/matrix.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a no-pod target emission mode, for example `python -m scripts.live_test.main --emit-targets-json <path> --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo ...`, that builds the selected live-test cases and writes target JSON without touching DB, RunPod, or worker launch paths.
2. Reuse the same resolver in normal `--variant prebuilt` execution so standalone checks and live consumer runs validate the same selected targets.
3. The target JSON contains selected case names, task types, route keys, selector/profile context, and resolved VibeComfy template ids only.
4. Do not include VibeComfy-derived asset metadata in this Reigh step, and do not import `vibecomfy` from `reigh-worker`.
5. Ensure the target set is derived from selected live-test cases/routes, not all VibeComfy ready templates.

### Step 5: Expose workflow source mode in named VibeComfy APIs (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py`, `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py`, VibeComfy tests)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a named source-mode enum or string field such as `python_composition`, `api_dict_wrapper`, or `json_reference` to the ready-template registry/loader surface that currently backs `ready_template_ids()` and `workflow_from_ready()`.
2. Implement source classification in `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/registry/ready_template.py` or an adjacent helper used by that registry, and expose it through a function such as `ready_template_source_info(template_id)`.
3. Use `vibecomfy/vibecomfy/cli_loader.py` only as a loader integration point; do not make callers infer source mode from compiled workflow dictionaries.
4. Add diagnostics that name the template id and source file when a selected app-active template is still an API-dict/JSON runtime wrapper.

### Step 6: Add VibeComfy-owned enrichment CLI (`vibecomfy/vibecomfy/commands/...`, VibeComfy asset helpers)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add a VibeComfy CLI command or subcommand that accepts Reigh’s target manifest JSON and emits an enriched validation manifest containing template source mode, schema-check inputs, expected model assets, asset categories/subdirectories, known URLs, and expected relative locations.
2. Support explicit output, for example `python -m vibecomfy.cli targets enrich --targets-json reigh-targets.json --out enriched-targets.json`.
3. Run this command with VibeComfy’s own Python environment: locally only when a VibeComfy checkout/python is explicitly supplied, or remotely inside `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv/bin/python` after the prebuilt VibeComfy checkout has been extracted/synced.
4. Keep `reigh-worker` as a JSON producer/consumer only. It may invoke the VibeComfy CLI as a subprocess when an explicit VibeComfy Python/check-out path is available, but it must not import VibeComfy modules.
5. Keep `runpod-lifecycle` as a JSON consumer/remote-command runner only. It must not import Reigh or VibeComfy package APIs locally.

### Step 7: Define enriched asset diagnostics and dry-run contracts (`vibecomfy` enrichment output, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Use the VibeComfy-enriched manifest as the source of expected model assets for selected templates.
2. Report missing assets with asset name, category/path, local paths checked through `extra_model_paths.yaml`, and known remediation when available.
3. Keep `check` non-mutating by default; it can print the exact reconcile command using the enriched manifest.
4. Define `rl prebuilt reconcile --dry-run` precisely: with `--asset-manifest` or an enriched `--targets-json`, print planned fetches; with a plain `--targets-json`, require explicit local enrichment options such as `--vibecomfy-python` and `--vibecomfy-root`, otherwise exit with a precise remediation command.
5. Non-dry-run reconcile may enrich remotely on a probe pod because credentials/network are allowed in that mode.

## Phase 3: Shared Probes And CLI Surface

### Step 8: Build reusable in-pod health probes (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. Refactor `verify_extracted_env()` into a shared function that accepts an already-connected SSH session, `PrebuiltEnvContract`, manifest, and optional enriched target/asset manifest data.
2. Probe the actual remote paths separately: `contract.runtime_venv_path` for worker and `contract.runtime_vibecomfy_path/.venv` for VibeComfy.
3. Check mount readability, required system packages (`ffmpeg`, `zstd`, `pv`), VibeComfy import, ComfyUI import, worker Torch/CUDA, VibeComfy Torch/CUDA, SageAttention only for `attention_profile=sage`, custom-node lock restore/list sanity, schema provider availability, `extra_model_paths.yaml`, selected source mode, selected node schemas, and selected model assets.
4. Return structured issue objects grouped by `environment`, `custom_nodes`, `workflow_source`, `schema`, `assets`, and `runtime_deferred`.

### Step 9: Add prebuilt check/status/cleanup/reconcile parser and dispatch (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Update the prebuilt dispatch map to include `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile` in addition to `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, and `list`.
2. Add parser entries and help text for all new subcommands.
3. Require `--dry-run` support for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`; dry-run must not require `RUNPOD_API_KEY` or provision a pod.
4. Add `--targets-json`, `--asset-manifest`, and explicit local enrichment options where needed. Do not add Reigh-owned `--case` or `--route-key` flags to `runpod-lifecycle`.
5. Add `--json`, `--attention-profile portable|sage`, `--gpu-type`, `--container-disk-gb`, and `--keep-pod` where applicable.

### Step 10: Implement standalone check and reconcile with clean package boundaries (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`)
**Scope:** Large
1. `rl prebuilt check` provisions a clearly named short-lived probe pod, attaches the selected volume, reads the manifest, extracts or inspects bundles as needed, runs the VibeComfy enrichment command remotely when a plain target manifest is supplied, runs shared in-pod probes, writes `env.health.json`, prints a concise result, and terminates by default.
2. `rl prebuilt check --dry-run` with a plain target manifest prints planned pod/volume/probe steps and says enrichment will occur remotely during non-dry-run; it does not need to print asset fetches.
3. `rl prebuilt reconcile` accepts an enriched asset manifest or runs remote VibeComfy enrichment first, then runs remote VibeComfy fetch/stage commands inside the pod checkout or stages known URLs directly into the model cache.
4. `rl prebuilt reconcile --dry-run` only prints asset fetch plans when given enriched metadata or explicit local enrichment options; otherwise it exits successfully or with code 2, as chosen by local CLI convention, with the exact enrichment command needed before retrying dry-run.
5. Neither command imports Reigh routing code or VibeComfy Python modules locally.
6. `--keep-pod` is explicit and includes pod id/reason in output when used.

### Step 11: Add prebuilt status and cleanup safety (`runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/cli.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/src/runpod_lifecycle/guard.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Add `rl prebuilt status --dry-run --json` that only lists pods matching explicit allowlisted prefixes: `reigh-livetest-builder-` and `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-`.
2. Add `rl prebuilt cleanup --older-than ... --dry-run --yes` that only terminates allowlisted prebuilt validation pods and never generic user pods.
3. Reuse existing prefix-pruning logic where practical, but keep the prebuilt allowlist visible and unit-tested.

## Phase 4: Live-Test Integration And Reports

### Step 12: Validate the actual consumer pod before queueing (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. After manifest read, hard-fail drift checks, extraction, worker git sync, VibeComfy git sync/delta install, and `_write_extra_model_paths_yaml()`, call the shared in-pod health probe directly on that same SSH session.
2. Generate the same Reigh target manifest used by `--emit-targets-json`, then run VibeComfy enrichment via the remote VibeComfy Python/checkout on the consumer pod before asset/schema checks.
3. Do not shell out to `rl prebuilt check` from inside `variant_prebuilt`; the CLI validates a separate probe pod and is only for standalone volume checks.
4. Abort before `register_worker_record()`, `launch_worker`, or `queue_matrix()` when health checks find environment, pure-Python source, schema, required input/connection, or asset failures.
5. Write the health summary, Reigh target manifest, and enriched VibeComfy manifest to the live-test run directory even on early failure when possible.

### Step 13: Add optional report metadata without breaking other variants (`reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/report.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/variant_prebuilt.py`, fresh/update call sites)
**Scope:** Small
1. Change `write_report(results, variant, pod_id, out_dir)` to accept an optional keyword-only metadata parameter, for example `write_report(..., metadata=None)`.
2. Have prebuilt pass volume id/name, data center, manifest path/hash, health report path/hash, target manifest path/hash, enriched manifest path/hash, selected profile, selected templates, GPU type, and check summary.
3. Leave fresh/update callers valid with no changes beyond the compatible function signature.

## Phase 5: Tests And Documentation

### Step 14: Add focused tests for builder, contracts, and CLI (`runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test that the builder sync command pins the requested worker Python version and fails before manifest write on observed mismatch.
2. Test health report JSON round trip, separate worker/VibeComfy interpreter records, missing asset diagnostics, and schema-check issue grouping.
3. Update existing prebuilt help tests so they expect all subcommands: `build`, `inspect`, `invalidate`, `list`, `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`.
4. Add no-credential dry-run tests for `check`, `status`, `cleanup`, and `reconcile`, including reconcile behavior with plain targets, enriched metadata, and explicit local enrichment options.
5. Add deterministic volume-selection tests for explicit volume name, explicit data center, multiple matching candidates, and canonical normalized names.

### Step 15: Add VibeComfy and live-test boundary tests (`vibecomfy/tests/...`, `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py`, `runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. Test `ready_template_source_info()` or equivalent on pure Python templates, API-dict wrapper templates, and JSON reference/corpus paths.
2. Test the VibeComfy enrichment CLI: target manifest in, enriched source/schema/asset manifest out.
3. Test `--emit-targets-json` produces a standalone target file for `z_image_turbo` and other selected cases without DB, RunPod, pod launch, or VibeComfy imports.
4. Test that Reigh target manifest generation resolves selected live-test cases/routes and excludes unrelated ready templates without importing `vibecomfy`.
5. Add a static/import-boundary test that fails if `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` directly imports `vibecomfy` for target or asset manifest generation.
6. Test that prebuilt health failure occurs before worker registration, launch, and matrix queueing.
7. Test that optional report metadata preserves existing fresh/update report behavior.
8. Test that `runpod-lifecycle reconcile` consumes resolved/enriched metadata and does not parse Reigh `--case` or `--route-key` flags.

### Step 16: Document the operational workflow (`runpod-lifecycle/README.md`, nearest `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test` docs, relevant VibeComfy docs)
**Scope:** Small
1. Document prebuilt vs fresh, default portable RTX 4090 behavior, optional larger GPU and SageAttention profiles, and canonical volume naming from RunPod `data_center_id` normalization.
2. Document `build`, `check`, `reconcile`, `status`, `cleanup`, `invalidate`, and `list`, including dry-run/no-credential behavior and the `--targets-json`/`--asset-manifest` handoff.
3. Document the explicit producer sequence: Reigh `--emit-targets-json`, optional VibeComfy local enrichment, then `rl prebuilt check` or `rl prebuilt reconcile`.
4. Document reconcile dry-run behavior for plain targets versus enriched manifests.
5. Document workflow onboarding: derive/convert/fork pure Python workflow, validate selected route/template schemas, verify assets, run cheap prebuilt check, run `z_image_turbo` smoke, then broaden to image/edit/upscale or video matrices.
6. State that app-active runtime workflow source must be pure Python; JSON/API-dict wrappers are allowed only as reference/import/corpus material.

## Phase 6: Validation

### Step 17: Run local verification
**Scope:** Small
1. Run targeted tests first: `pytest runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_prebuilt.py runpod-lifecycle/tests/test_cli.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_variant_prebuilt.py reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/tests/test_dry_run_smoke.py` plus the VibeComfy source/enrichment tests.
2. Run broader affected suites after targeted tests pass.

### Step 18: Battle-test on RunPod
**Scope:** Large
1. Generate the target manifest first: `python -m scripts.live_test.main --emit-targets-json <reigh-targets.json> --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo`.
2. Discover the actual current portable prebuilt volume and data center from RunPod instead of hard-coding the historical example.
3. Run `rl prebuilt check --targets-json <reigh-targets.json>` against the selected portable 4090 profile and confirm remote VibeComfy enrichment plus `env.health.json` evidence are written.
4. Run the first remote smoke gate with `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/main.py --variant prebuilt --backend vibecomfy --case z_image_turbo` using the selected volume/profile.
5. Confirm pods launched by check/smoke are terminated unless intentionally preserved with `--keep-pod`, and record any preserved pod id and reason.
6. After base health and smoke pass, run broader image/edit/upscale or video matrix validation as a follow-up gate, not as the first blocker.

## Execution Order
1. Enforce builder worker Python version before trusting or expanding manifest/health evidence.
2. Add deterministic volume selection before wiring new commands that depend on volume/profile names.
3. Add Reigh `--emit-targets-json` so standalone validation has an explicit artifact producer.
4. Add VibeComfy source-mode API and VibeComfy-owned enrichment CLI before asset reconciliation.
5. Add parser/dispatch/dry-run behavior for new CLI verbs with precise plain-target versus enriched-manifest contracts.
6. Wire remote VibeComfy enrichment and shared in-pod probes into `variant_prebuilt` after target metadata and probes are stable.
7. Add report metadata compatibility and docs after command/output shape is settled.

## Validation Order
1. Start with builder Python enforcement and dry-run/no-credential CLI tests.
2. Run unit tests for target JSON emission, health report serialization, volume selection, source classification, VibeComfy enrichment, target-manifest handoff, asset diagnostics, import-boundary checks, and cleanup allowlists.
3. Run live-test integration tests proving early failure before queueing.
4. Run broader affected local tests.
5. Generate `reigh-targets.json`, then run real `rl prebuilt check --targets-json ...` on RunPod.
6. Run real `z_image_turbo` prebuilt smoke and verify report evidence plus pod cleanup.

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 5,
    "idea": "# Prebuilt VibeComfy Validation Environment\n\nWe are migrating Reigh's app-active AI generation paths from direct Wan2GP execution to VibeComfy-backed pure-Python workflow templates. The user wants absolute feature parity with the Wan2GP path, actual RunPod validation, and tooling that prevents future agents/developers from rediscovering the same problems manually.\n\nCurrent reality:\n- `reigh-worker` branch: `megaplan/vibecomfy-sprint-09-control-rail-travel-matrix`\n- `vibecomfy` branch: `megaplan/production-parity-templates`\n- `runpod-lifecycle` branch: `megaplan/prebuilt-validation-env`\n- Existing prebuilt RunPod validation volume has already proven useful but is not yet a clean, repeatable developer experience.\n- Fresh installs repeatedly hit slow or flaky setup problems: network-volume stale file handles, missing system packages such as `ffmpeg`, missing custom-node assets, missing model directories in `extra_model_paths.yaml`, optional attention backends such as SageAttention, and pod lifecycle sprawl.\n- A recent local fix in `reigh-worker` moved fresh/update VibeComfy virtualenvs from `/workspace/.../.venv` to `/opt/reigh-vibecomfy-live-test-venv` so package installs happen on local disk instead of the RunPod network volume. That was tested with 152 local tests and pushed.\n\nGoal:\nBuild and validate a robust, reusable prebuilt environment path for Reigh VibeComfy validation. It should make the rest of the parity work easier and safer, not add a rigid second source of truth.\n\nThe target design should:\n1. Provide a standard prebuilt environment for 4090-class RunPod validation.\n   - Prefer RTX 4090 as the normal target because that is the user's realistic production/developer bar.\n   - Keep the design able to run on larger GPUs when explicitly requested.\n   - Make the environment portable when advanced kernels are unavailable, while still allowing a SageAttention-capable profile if it can be installed and validated cleanly.\n2. Keep the VibeComfy workflow source pure Python.\n   - No app-active workflow should rely on executing JSON blobs as the workflow implementation.\n   - JSON may exist only as reference/corpus/import material, not the runtime source of truth.\n3. Make environment dependencies explicit but not burdensome.\n   - Capture system packages, Python version, CUDA/Torch profile, ComfyUI/VibeComfy commits, custom node lockfile state, extra model paths, optional acceleration profile, and model asset expectations in one generated or easily maintained manifest.\n   - Avoid forcing developers to update a fragile hand-written manifest every time a workflow changes. Prefer deriving information from templates, contracts, lockfiles, and validation output.\n4. Make validation fail early.\n   - Before running expensive workflows, check the environment can import VibeComfy, restore/list custom nodes, read required model paths, validate node input schemas against the installed node classes, and resolve model assets.\n   - The onboarding/check path should catch missing required connections and missing required node inputs before RunPod execution.\n   - If a model asset is missing, the tool should report the exact expected asset and where it looked. If a URL is known, it should be able to reconcile/fetch or print a precise remediation.\n5. Make pod lifecycle safe.\n   - Never kill unrelated user pods.\n   - Tag/label/name pods launched by this tooling clearly.\n   - Provide a safe cleanup/status command that only targets pods launched for this validation flow.\n6. Integrate with current Reigh live-test tools instead of creating a parallel universe.\n   - Existing code lives primarily in `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test`, `runpod-lifecycle`, and `vibecomfy`.\n   - Use the existing live-test matrix and reports.\n   - Prebuilt should become the easy/default validation path when the volume/profile exists; fresh should remain a fallback and a way to validate bootstrap correctness.\n7. Be documented for agents and developers.\n   - Update the relevant README/skill/docs so a future agent knows when to use the prebuilt path, how to build/update it, how to validate it, and what evidence counts.\n   - Document the \"workflow onboarding\" checklist: derive/convert/fork Python workflow, validate node schemas, validate assets, run cheap env checks, run smoke, run matrix, record contract evidence.\n\nExpected implementation shape:\n- Improve or add CLIs/scripts around prebuilt env build/check/update/reconcile/status/cleanup.\n- Add tests for the new behavior.\n- Where possible, use existing abstractions in VibeComfy: template registry, model asset extraction, node lock restore, node schema inspection, workflow validation/doctor/converter tools.\n- If missing abstractions are found, add the smallest useful abstraction that removes repeated manual friction.\n- Keep changes scoped and avoid unrelated refactors.\n\nValidation requirements:\n- Local unit tests for any changed Python modules.\n- At least one remote RunPod prebuilt environment health check.\n- At least one VibeComfy workflow smoke run using the prebuilt environment.\n- Evidence should be written to the existing live-test report structure or a similarly discoverable report path.\n- Pods launched for validation must be terminated at the end unless intentionally preserved and clearly documented.\n\nKnown useful references already produced:\n- Prebuilt volume that previously passed image/edit/upscale batch: `reigh-livetest-prebuilt-portable-eur-no-1` (`h6ln28obus`) in `EUR-NO-1`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T013030Z/report.json` passed 8/8 image/edit/upscale cases.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260513T012010Z/report.json` passed `z_image_turbo`.\n- Prior successful report: `reigh-worker/scripts/live_test/runs/20260512T204040Z/report.json` passed LTX control matrix 4/4.\n- Do not assume those are enough; this task is about making the prebuilt environment path systematic and then using it.\n\nImportant safety:\n- Do not print secrets from `.env`, process environments, or SSH `ps e` output.\n- Do not kill unrelated pods. Only clean up pods clearly launched by this flow.\n- Preserve dirty user changes unless they are directly part of this work.\n\nSuccess criteria:\n- The repo has a clear, tested prebuilt-environment flow that future agents can discover and use by default for VibeComfy validation.\n- The flow makes it obvious whether a workflow is blocked by environment setup, missing assets, invalid node inputs, or runtime workflow logic.\n- Documentation explains both the \"why\" and the commands succinctly.\n- The tooling is actually battle-tested by running at least one prebuilt RunPod health/smoke validation and recording the result.",
